## HYP-1862: q(x)=x-1/x is the reciprocal counterterm for triangular log cutoffs (codex-2026-05-31-S394)
**Status:** OPEN; calculus identity proved, broader structural role conjectural.
**What:** For `p(x)=min(x,1/x)` on `[0,2]`, write `p(x)=1/x+q(x)` with `q(x)=x-1/x` on `(0,1]` and `q=0` on `[1,2]`. Then `q` is a finite-part counterterm that cancels the divergent negative log-ray of `1/x` and replaces it by the triangular mass `1/2`.
**Evidence:** With cutoff `epsilon`, `int_epsilon^2 dx/x=ln(2/epsilon)` and `int_epsilon^1 (x-1/x)dx=1/2+ln(epsilon)-epsilon^2/2`, so the sum tends to `1/2+ln2`. In log-coordinate `x=e^u`, the patched density is `e^(2u)` on `u<=0` and `1` on `0<=u<=ln2`. Bare `q0=x-1/x` is anti-reciprocal and has Mellin transform `-2/(s^2-1)`. A literal `30-60-90` triangle cannot keep the exact `ln2` remainder without a `q`-defect.
**Predictions:** Classify alternate triangle decompositions by `q`-defects: area debt, endpoint jump, slope jump, and Mellin residue. Compare `q0=x-1/x` with the Cayley shadow `Q(Q(x))=-1/x` as two forms of reciprocal inversion residue.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1862-hyperbola-triangle-q-counterterm.md`, `04-computation/hyperbola_triangle_q_s394.py`, `05-knowledge/results/hyperbola_triangle_q_s394.out`, `07-reflections/hyperbola-triangle-q-s394.md`.

## HYP-1863: q-gauges expose the finite part behind logarithmic constants (codex-2026-05-31-S395)
**Status:** OPEN structural extension; base identity proved in THM-368.
**What:** Many `log(2)` appearances should be revisited as finite-part decompositions `dx/x + q`, where `dx/x` is invariant log measure and `q` is a finite packet or reciprocal-odd counterterm. In the hyperbola-triangle model, `q_ct=x-1/x` is the structural object because `q_ct(1/x)=-q_ct(x)`.
**Evidence:** THM-368 proves `int_0^1 x dx + int_1^2 dx/x = FP int_0^2(1/x+q_L) dx = lim_eps [int_eps^2 dx/x + int_eps^1(x-1/x) dx] = 1/2+log(2)`. Equal-area triangle gauges `q_L=x/L^2` preserve the finite part; a 30-degree equal-area triangle has `L=3^(1/4)` and log cutoff `log(3)/4`.
**Predictions:** Anti-palindromic tournament polynomials are discrete versions of `x-1/x`; changing `L` in q-gauges is a one-dimensional renormalization-group translation in log coordinate; existing rapidity lattice constants should split into log intervals plus q-packet finite parts.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1863-q-gauge-logarithmic-constants.md`, THM-368, HYP-1862, `04-computation/hyperbola_triangle_q_gauge_s395.py`, `05-knowledge/results/hyperbola_triangle_q_gauge_s395.out`, `07-reflections/hyperbola-triangle-q-gauge-s395.md`.

## HYP-1864: Logarithmic area remainders are zero-mass q-transports (codex-2026-05-31-S396)
**Status:** OPEN; exact base example plus exploratory generalization.
**What:** In mixed polynomial/reciprocal area decompositions, logarithmic remainders survive when the finite geometric carve is tuned so the residual correction `q` has zero mass; the log scale then appears in Mellin/log derivatives of `q` rather than in its area.
**Evidence:** `log_triangle_q_decomposition_s396.py` shows that for `f=x` on `[0,1]` and `f=1/x` on `[1,2]`, subtracting a ramp `a*x` gives `H(x-1)/x + q_a`, where `q_a=(1-a)x` on `[0,1]`, `q_a=-a*x` on `[1,2]`, and `integral q_a=1/2-2a`. The unique log-preserving ramp is `a=1/4`, so `q=3x/4` on `[0,1]` and `q=-x/4` on `[1,2]`, with moments `(1-2^n)/(n+2)` and Mellin derivative `M'(1)=-ln(2)/2`. The same audit shows the literal 30-60-90 interpretation is Euclidean-area incompatible with a span from `0` to `2` and remainder `ln(2)`.
**Predictions:** Similar `ln` constants in rapidity and natural-operation threads should admit zero-mass q-transport models whose Mellin derivatives recover log ratios; checking `integral q=0` should identify the correct finite carve normalization.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1864-log-area-q-transport.md`, `04-computation/log_triangle_q_decomposition_s396.py`, `05-knowledge/results/log_triangle_q_decomposition_s396.out`, `07-reflections/log-triangle-q-decomposition-s396.md`.

## HYP-1866: LRC gap-debt product forbids simultaneous zero (codex-2026-05-31-S398)
**Status:** OPEN; exact ladder products, weighted norm still conjectural.
**What:** For an LRC speed set, let `Gap_A=max_gap/(1/n)` and let `Debt_2` be exposed endpoint debt, first by raw unprotected endpoint count and later by quotient-layer weights. The conjectural repair-branch invariant is `Gap_A*Debt_2 >= c(n)>0`. Boundary-only systems have `Gap_A=0, Debt_2>0`; a counterexample would need `Gap_A=0, Debt_2=0` simultaneously.

Initial segments at `n=14,16,18` show the boundary branch separately: zero gap but positive endpoint debt.

Then THM-357/THM-365/THM-366/THM-367 should combine into an adelic product obstruction: closing the Archimedean gap forces denominator debt rather than annihilating the witness.

## HYP-1880: LRC endpoint debt is Bruhat-Tits descent (codex-2026-05-31-S410)
**Status:** OPEN; exact local p-depth identities plus exploratory global proof target.
**What:** Attach to every LRC endpoint `t` the p-adic denominator-depth vector `d_p(t)=v_p(denominator(t))` for primes `p|n`. This is the product of rank-one Bruhat-Tits tree shadows. A counterexample would be an all-protected endpoint core with zero divergence in this product tree; the hypothesis is that strict protection and primitivity force positive divergence.
**Evidence:** `lrc_bruhat_tits_descent_s410.py` verifies the exact unit-gate and gate-export ledgers. THM-360 says the unit layer at depth `(v_p(n))` is killed only by an `n`-gate. If `v=n*q`, every endpoint owned by `v` has depth `2*v_p(n)+v_p(q)` for `p|n`, because `n*m+/-1` is a p-adic unit. For `n=14`, the initial tight row exposes `{2:1,7:1}` while the S380 14-multiple ladder exports exposure to `{2:2,7:2}` and `{2:4,7:2}`. For `n=16`, gates export `{2:4}` to `{2:8}` and beyond, matching THM-367's pure dyadic radial law.
**Predictions:** The `n=14` proof should use a product-tree potential on the `2`- and `7`-adic factors; the `n=16` proof should use a one-prime BT flow with residue branch labels. Every labelled endpoint cycle from THM-365 should have nonzero BT divergence unless it uses a super-gate, and super-gates export debt to deeper children.

## HYP-1893: LRC coarse/fine dichotomy at q = n (oracle-2026-05-31-S18)
**Status:** EXPLORATORY structural framing; coarse half PROVED (Lean THM-369), fine half open. Convergent with codex HYP-1855.
**What:** The rational time-line splits at denominator `q=n`. COARSE regime `2≤q≤n`: loneliness at any reduced `a/q` is a pure divisibility question — `q∉D(V) ⇒ a/q lonely` (open if `q<n`, boundary if `q=n`), where `D(V)={q:∃i, q∣v i}`. FINE regime `q>n`: the safe residue band `[q/n, q−q/n]` is nonempty/wide, so `a/q` can be lonely even when `q∤v i`; loneliness depends on whether every `v i·a mod q` lands in the band (the micro-staircase regime). A counterexample must cover all coarse denominators by divisibility AND survive the fine band condition at every `q>n`; the two pull oppositely across the `q=n` seam (cf. gap-floor HYP-1851). UV/IR split: IR=arithmetic/sieve (closed, Lean THM-369), UV=equidistribution/analysis (open frontier).
**Evidence:** Coarse half is THM-369 / THM-366 (proved). Reframing: coarse sieve = covering prescribed subgroups of `ℤ/lcm(1..n)` by the `n−1` speed residues (n=14: `lcm=360360`).
**Predictions:** All tightness/counterexample tension lives at the `q=n` seam where the band degenerates to a point and divisibility = band-membership; explains why tight examples (THM-358) concentrate at unit endpoints. A fine-regime band predicate should match codex's endpoint-debt (HYP-1855/1858).
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1893-lrc-coarse-fine-dichotomy-q-n.md`, `07-reflections/lrc-denominator-sieve-lean-and-two-regimes-s18.md`, `01-canon/theorems/THM-369-lrc-sieve-lean-formalization.md`, THM-366, HYP-1850, HYP-1851, HYP-1855.

## HYP-1895: LRC is a two-neighbor cut problem in a circular tournament flow (oracle-2026-05-31-S22)
**Status:** OPEN; generated by S22 distance/tournament audit.
**What:** For an LRC speed set, include the stationary runner as a marked vertex in the circular order at time `t`. Loneliness is exactly the condition that the predecessor and successor gaps adjacent to the stationary vertex are both at least `1/n`. A counterexample is therefore a labelled circular-order/tournament flow that keeps one of these two marked cut gaps blocked in every state while paying all endpoint and coarse divisor rows.

Many positive-gap rows have no moving runner for which the stationary runner is the nearest neighbor, so the unmarked nearest-neighbor graph is insufficient.
**Predictions:** Track labelled predecessor/successor pairs of the stationary vertex as search states; translate endpoint-protection events into crossings of the two threshold gates around `0`; add marked bracket rows to the S21 Hall/IP certificate; keep tournament features labelled because score histograms alone miss LRC branch distinctions.

**Status:** OPEN; exact equivalence atlas plus conjectural functorial proof target.
**What:** Exact LRC formulations should all factor through the same finite object: a one-parameter subgroup, forbidden neighborhoods of coordinate subtori, and the protected boundary incidence of equality states. Distance-graph multiplier colorings, subtorus/cube avoidance, view obstruction, zonotope covering radius, spectra, IP rows, and Bruhat-Tits frontier mass are useful to the extent that they preserve positive gaps and unprotected endpoints. This refines HYP-1900's labelled incidence-core claim by naming the anti-Bohr protected boundary as the invariant to transport.
**Evidence:** `lrc_analogy_atlas_s450.py` rechecks exact n=14 boundary examples: initial `n=14` is boundary-only with `6` unprotected endpoints; the seven-ladder is positive-gap with `gap/th=5/924` and `84` unprotected endpoints; the S380 gate ladder is positive-gap with `gap/th=5/1848` and `168` unprotected endpoints. The web pass identifies current preprint progress through thirteen runners, making `n=14` the next frontier denominator if those results are taken as the working state.
**Predictions:** Any analogy that cannot identify the image of an unprotected endpoint is poetic but not a proof engine. A serious `n=14` certificate should be readable as endpoint peel, IP dual rows, and zonotope covering debt. Zeckendorf/Ostrowski analogues become useful only when they give a no-adjacent normal form for endpoint debt.

The golden irrational rotation supplies the exact anti-Bohr model because Ostrowski numeration becomes Zeckendorf when every continued-fraction digit is `1`.

## HYP-1903: LRC has a two-nearest-neighbor tournament lift (codex-2026-05-31-S431)
**Status:** OPEN; generated by S431 neighbor/tournament lift.
**What:** At time `t`, the LRC configuration `{0} union {v_i t mod 1}` should be studied as an incomplete round tournament plus a two-nearest-neighbor digraph. **Predictions:** Add `(lonely_gap, second_zero_gap, pairwise_min_gap, antipodal_count, collision_count, two_nearest_private_leaf_count)` to LRC feature vectors; implement two-nearest core peeling; reinterpret sporadic tight examples as residue polygons with multiplicities; seek a circular version of THM-354 where bad zero-bracket cuts are condensation boundaries in the two-nearest protection graph.

## HYP-1904: LRC admits a runner-distance tournament lift (codex-2026-05-31-S452)
**Status:** OPEN; exact static two-neighbor reformulation plus exploratory tournament-distance proof program.

**Predictions:** Build chamber automata of circular orders plus safe-gap masks; use Zeckendorf/path quotients for mask normal forms; use score sequences, close-degree histograms, directed triangles, and good-cut analogues as tournament-style LRC search/pruning features; recast unsafe gaps as an edge-cover/Hall obstruction.

## HYP-1905: LRC first-even denominators are parity-seam export problems (codex-2026-05-31-S453)
**Status:** OPEN; exact first-doubling seam plus exploratory proof route.
**What:** The first row step `m odd -> 2m` is not ordinary doubling. THM-371 proves `U(2m)~=U(m)` by unique odd lift and `phi(2m)=phi(m)`, while matching pairs gain the extra twin `pairs(2m)=2*pairs(m)+1`. THM-378 refines the tournament side: vertex doubling is a double-round-robin voltage lift whose hidden sheet parities, not quotient scores, carry the old edge data. For LRC, first-even denominators open nonunit quotient room without new unit witnesses; repairs using that room should export endpoint debt.
**Evidence:** `lrc_tournament_first_doubling_seam_s453.py` prints the exact seam ledger and first-even row-parent ladders: `n=14` has `gap/th=5/924,debt=84,product=5/11`, `n=18` has `1/176,176,1`, and `n=22` has `2/561,260,520/561`. The same script shows SC blowup forces universal twin score splits while lex blowup magnifies hierarchy. S501c shows signed double-round-robin doublings have `2^binom(n-1,2)` sheet-flip gauge classes, matching the fixed-base tiling cube dimension.
**Predictions:** Prove `n=14` by inherited odd-lift unit rows plus row-parent/product-depth debt export; use `n=18` as the next first-even square-core test; keep `n=16` separate as the pure dyadic row lab; add first-seam flags and odd-lift labels to LRC search features.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1905-lrc-first-doubling-parity-seam.md`, THM-371, THM-378, HYP-1881, HYP-1891, HYP-1904, HYP-1910, HYP-1920, `04-computation/lrc_tournament_first_doubling_seam_s453.py`, `04-computation/double_round_robin_blowup_s501.py`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_tournament_first_doubling_seam_s453.out`, `05-knowledge/results/double_round_robin_blowup_s501.out`, `07-reflections/lrc-tournament-first-doubling-seam-s453.md`, `07-reflections/double-round-robin-vertex-doubling-s501.md`.

## HYP-1920: The n=14 LRC proof has a Zeckendorf-shell certificate (codex-2026-05-31-S460)
**Status:** OPEN; exact local cover-family factorization plus exploratory proof route.
**What:** Label each speed column by its Zeckendorf support, i.e. an independent set in the Fibonacci path. In this coordinate, the `14`-gate endpoint invoice factors as a forced odd fan plus one even bridge: all minimum lower covers are `{1,3,5,7,9,11,13} union {e}` for exactly one `e` in `{2,4,6,8,10,12}`. A proof should quotient these six even bridges as a local Fibonacci-cube fiber, then show global LRC rows put positive dual weight on every fiber element.
**Evidence:** `lrc_zeckendorf_bridge_s460.py` enumerates all local lower-cover families for `n=14,15,16`. It finds `n=14` exact size `8` with `6` minimum covers; `n=15` exact size `10` with `8` covers; and `n=16` exact size `9` with a unique cover. It also identifies the denominator shell `14=F6+F1`, `15=F6+F2`, `16=F6+F3`, `18=F6+F4`, `21=F7`, and rewrites S380 owner debt with top Zeckendorf index `F11`.
**Predictions:** Coarse denominator, primitivity, and owner-debt rows should break the local even-bridge symmetry. Future feature extractors should add Zeckendorf top index, digit load, support overlap, and no-carry/carry status beside `2^h*odd_core` and Bruhat-Tits depth.

## HYP-1921: LRC counterexamples require a labelled two-neighbor handoff tournament (codex-2026-05-31-S461)
**Status:** OPEN; exact endpoint-neighbor audit plus proof-technology proposal.
**What:** The scalar nearest-runner function loses the boundary handoff data needed for a proof. A counterexample would require a labelled directed core where every threshold endpoint owner has a strict protector and no two-sided boundary witness remains. The minimal lift records left/right nearest neighbors, second distinct nearest distance, threshold owner set, strict protector set, side, slack, and denominator depth.

It finds many fragile single-handoff rows (`128`, `710`, `1420`, and `164` respectively), while the compressed pairwise protection-majority tournament on speeds is complete and transitive in every audited case. Thus the proof-relevant tournament object is not a speed-level tournament but the labelled endpoint handoff graph, whose SCCs can remain dense while witnesses survive.
**Predictions:** Single-handoff rows should become private pivots in an endpoint-transfer matrix; redundant handoffs should collapse into compatible repair packets; a useful LRC Omega graph should put labelled handoffs as vertices and conflicts as edges. For `n=14`, HYP-1920's even bridge fiber should eventually expose a left/right witness or export Zeckendorf-height debt.

**What:** Include the stationary runner and study all pairwise distances in `{0} union V`. ## HYP-1931: Tournament Analysis is metric lifting plus wall-crossing (oracle-2026-06-01-S23)
**Status:** OPEN; generated by S23 metric-lift audit.
**What:** Tournament Analysis is the pipeline `raw pairwise/geometric data -> metric/sensor -> binary comparator plus tie-break path -> tournament -> invariants and wall-crossing`. Rank lifts assign one scalar per vertex and usually collapse to transitive tournaments; analyzer lifts decide edges by pair-specific data and preserve cyclic structure.
**Evidence:** `tournament_analysis_metric_lifts_s23.py` tests basketball pass flux, circle-runner metrics, cuboid/sphere/simplex-style point-cloud metrics, metric edge-switches, and LRC witness rows. Score lifts were transitive in `316/316` sampled continuous states, while edge-local/switch lifts were transitive in only `1/290`. Exact clusters show `phase-halfturn = pivot-area` and `anchor-arc-far = anchor-chord-far`. S454 sharpens this into a switchboard layer: rank shadows are transitive in `172/172` states, analyzer shadows in only `31/672`, with mean analyzer live-edge count `22.95/28`. S471 packages the same process as pairwise observable plus switch functional plus tie path. S500 canonized the switchboard, runner-clock, and semicircle pieces as THM-372, THM-373, and THM-374.
**Predictions:** Every metric-to-tournament pipeline should declare whether it is a rank lift or analyzer lift; continuous problems should be studied as wall-crossing paths in `{0,1}^{binom(n,2)}`; future LRC feature extractors should record both rank shadows and analyzer shadows, with the difference measuring cyclic information beyond scalar ordering. Standard switchboard/gauge features should include live edges, wall count, Hamming diameter, H range, SCC range, and exact path clusters.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1931-tournament-analysis-metric-lifts.md`, THM-372, THM-373, THM-374, HYP-1895, HYP-1900, HYP-1932, HYP-1940, HYP-1941, `04-computation/tournament_analysis_metric_lifts_s23.py`, `05-knowledge/results/tournament_analysis_metric_lifts_s23.out`, `07-reflections/tournament-analysis-metric-lifts-s23.md`, `04-computation/tournament_analysis_switchboard_s454.py`, `05-knowledge/results/tournament_analysis_switchboard_s454.out`, `07-reflections/tournament-analysis-switchboard-s454.md`, `04-computation/tournament_analysis_framework_s471.py`, `05-knowledge/results/tournament_analysis_framework_s471.out`, `07-reflections/tournament-analysis-framework-s471.md`.

## HYP-1932: Tournament Analysis switchboards are the primary relation layer (codex-2026-06-01-S454)
**Status:** OPEN; generated by S454 switchboard audit.
**What:** The pairwise comparator family itself should be treated as the main Tournament Analysis object. A switchboard is one bit-channel `c_ij(x)` for every unordered pair, plus a fixed Hamiltonian-path tie-break, producing a path through the tournament cube. Rankers summarize objects; switchboards summarize relations. THM-372 proves the finite switchboard construction is well-defined.

Rank shadows are transitive in `172/172` states; analyzer shadows are transitive in `31/672`. LRC safe-distance switches distinguish initial skeletons (`0` cycles for n14/n16 initial) from structured ladders (`93` and `139` cycles).
**Predictions:** Tournament Analysis artifacts should declare switchboard type and record live edges, total wall crossings, max step flip, Hamming diameter, H range, SCC range, and exact path clusters. For LRC, keep the marked lonely bracket and at least one pairwise switchboard shadow because scalar safety can hide cyclic crowding.

## HYP-1940: Tournament Analysis is a gauge functor from pairwise data to tournament movies (codex-2026-05-31-S480)
**Status:** OPEN; methodological hypothesis and feature-engineering program.
**What:** Tournament Analysis studies maps `(labels, pairwise observables M_ij(t), gauge g, tie path P) -> T_g(t)`. The tournament is not usually inherent in the data; it is produced by choosing a binary gauge. For symmetric metrics, a threshold/annulus predicate flips edges over a fixed Hamiltonian path, giving a direct `N choose 2` bridge back to the repo's tiling cube. THM-372/373 formalize the finite switchboard and runner-clock cases.
**Evidence:** `tournament_analysis_metric_lifts_s480.py` compares basketball pass-flow, runner chord metrics, kinetic pull-apart gauges, and sphere/cube/simplex point-cloud gauges. On speeds `(0,1,4,5,6,7,11)`, vertex-score gauges stay transitive, while chord threshold and chord annulus switches produce strongly connected cyclic tournament movies with Hamiltonian-path counts up to `157` and `159`. Point-cloud distance switches similarly produce cyclic SCC-one tournaments where projection/centroid gauges are total orders.
**Predictions:** Useful Tournament Analysis reports should include gauge, tie path, flip surfaces, and invariant movies. LRC is marked Tournament Analysis: the stationary runner provides the mark, S22/S431 are bracket/two-nearest gauges, and pairwise chord-switch gauges should expose global crowding/cycle structure invisible to the scalar lonely gap.

**Status:** OPEN; framework hypothesis with computational probe.

**Status:** OPEN; generated by alternating n=14/n=18 Tournament Analysis feedback.

## HYP-1951: the runner tournament-clock realizes a fixed circular menu in G_n (oracle-2026-06-01-S24)
**Status:** OPEN; runner-clock wall and semicircle pieces canonized as THM-373/THM-374; fixed-menu claim remains exploratory.
**What:** Lift n runners (speeds s_0<...<s_{n-1}) to a tournament by the phase comparator `i->j iff frac((s_j-s_i)t) in (0,1/2)` (ties->base path). THM-373 proves that as t sweeps [0,1) the tournament T(t) is a closed finite walk in G_n with wall times `m/(2|s_i-s_j|)`. CLAIMS: (a) FIXED MENU: the realizable iso-classes/H-values depend only on n, not the speeds (n=5: H in {1,9,11,15}, 4 of 12; n=6: {1,17,23,23,41,45}, 6 of 56) -- the image is exactly the "circular" (points-on-circle half-turn) tournaments; (b) THM-374 proves transitive(H=1) <=> all runners in an open semicircle <=> a 1/2-gap; S26b refines the old monotone-meter slogan: H=1 is exact, while H>1 is correlated with spread but not a scalar max-gap meter; S506 adds the marked LRC reading where high-H/even-spread cells can carry many local safe gaps; (c) the LRC-extremal set (0,1,...,n-1) gives the MINIMAL clock (fewest cells/iso-classes); (d) GEOMETRY constrains the image -- the basketball flux lift (arbitrary pairwise data) reaches tournaments outside the circular menu, so only circle-geometry restricts to 4 classes.
**Evidence:** `tournament_clock_s24.py`, `tournament_clock_identify_s24.py`, `tournament_clock_basketball_gap_s24.py` (+ .out). n=5 menu = {transitive, (1,1,2,3,3), (1,2,2,2,3), regular rotational R_5(H=15)}; semicircle<=>transitive confirmed 0 mismatches; basketball teams gave H=5,13 (outside menu). S502 overlays the half-turn clock with LRC endpoint walls: initial unit witnesses are clock walls, while hard n=14/n=18 lonely intervals are short corridors through adjacent half-turn cells. S506 adds exact `H`/gap/stationary/lonely-vertex audits: `H=1` is the open-semicircle endpoint, but initial LRC witnesses `t=1/n` for total `n=5..9` have every vertex lonely and high H.
**Predictions:** The circular menu = tournaments realizable by n points on a circle under the half-turn rule (a known small family); the alpha=1/k comparator family's "all-in-arc-1/k" cells = loneliness cells at threshold 1/k; speed sets with the same difference-relation lattice share the clock signature; hard LRC rows need a half-turn clock-corridor vector plus marked safe-gap and pressure data in addition to anchored endpoint data.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1951-runner-tournament-clock-circular-menu.md`, THM-373, THM-374, HYP-1967, HYP-1970, HYP-1971, `07-reflections/tournament-clock-runner-walks-in-Gn-s24.md`, `07-reflections/tournament-analysis-metric-lifts-s23.md`, `04-computation/lrc_tournament_clock_overlay_s502.py`, `07-reflections/lrc-tournament-clock-overlay-s502.md`, `04-computation/h_loneliness_meter_s506.py`, `05-knowledge/results/h_loneliness_meter_s506.out`, `07-reflections/h-as-loneliness-meter-s506.md`, HYP-1931, HYP-1850, THM-369.

## HYP-1952: n=18 LRC admits a square-core packet certificate (codex-2026-06-01-S493)
**Status:** OPEN; exact n=18 bridge/product/pressure certificate proposal.

The two bridge choices `6` and `12` cover the same six residual owner-18 endpoints, so they must be distinguished by global owner rows and product-depth packets, not by the local gate row.
**Evidence:** `lrc_n18_abstract_certificate_s493.py` finds residual symmetric difference `0` between bridges `6` and `12`; a signed owner-row shadow on rows `7,8,9,10,11,12,16`; conserved ladder products `1`; invariant normalized packet `96*(0,2)+16*(0,3)+64*(4,2)` across row-parent/gate/double-gate ladders; and no strict pressure SCC for `k1`, `k2`, or deficit pressure in the sampled `n=18` rows.
**Predictions:** A finite row-weight vector on the global owner rows plus square-core packet rows should charge both bridge signs unless a pressure SCC appears. Nonmultiple gap improvements should not threaten the proof unless they also erase endpoint/product debt and survive pressure-leaf peeling.

## HYP-1953: additive basis theorems form a coverage-density-normal-form spectrum (codex-2026-06-01-S494)
**Status:** OPEN; cross-problem synthesis plus exact small probe.
**What:** Goldbach, Helfgott's ternary Goldbach theorem, Hardy-Littlewood's Goldbach heuristic, Fermat's polygonal number theorem, and Zeckendorf's theorem are all representation-hypergraph statements. The shared profile is `(atom set, arity, representation count, local obstruction product, normal-form carry debt)`.
**Evidence:** `additive_basis_normal_forms_s494.py` compares Goldbach pair counts to a rough unordered Hardy-Littlewood singular-series heuristic, counts ternary prime triples for odd targets, verifies polygonal bounded-cover maxima for `k=3..8` up to `300`, and introduces Zeckendorf carry debt on Goldbach pairs. Some prime pairs have zero Zeckendorf carry debt, e.g. `50=13+37`, `100=11+89`, and `5000=673+4327`.
**Predictions:** Hardy-Littlewood singular series and Zeckendorf carry debt are dual local corrections: p-adic residue debt versus path-carry debt. Goldbach-like searches should record normal-form compatibility in addition to count and balance; Helfgott's ternary lift should be read as a dimension jump from brittle edges to averaged surfaces; Fermat polygonal is the bounded-arity sanity check.

## HYP-1960: LRC pressure searches return DAG certificates before they return counterexamples (codex-2026-06-01-S500)
**Status:** OPEN; generated by pressure-DAG audit.

**Predictions:** Pressure searches should output topological peel ledgers. Endpoint-private rows and pressure-DAG sources should pair into a dual certificate. THM-379/THM-380 now formalize the payoff: a pressure DAG excludes any nonempty terminal endpoint core whose strict protection incidences are pressure-realized. ## HYP-1962: Goldbach, Helfgott, polygonal numbers, and Zeckendorf lie on one additive-basis entropy ladder (codex-2026-06-01-S501)
**Status:** OPEN; representation-hypergraph synthesis with finite probe.
**What:** Goldbach, Helfgott's ternary theorem, Hardy-Littlewood, Fermat's polygonal number theorem, and Zeckendorf should be classified as representation-hypergraph regimes. Goldbach asks for nonempty prime-pair edges; Helfgott proves nonempty ternary prime hyperedges; Hardy-Littlewood predicts representation entropy through a singular series; Fermat polygonal numbers give bounded-depth additive bases; Zeckendorf gives a unique path-independent no-carry support.
**Evidence:** `goldbach_polygonal_zeckendorf_bridge_s501.py` compares these systems through finite probes. Goldbach pair counts stay positive and roughly Hardy-Littlewood-shaped through `2000`; ternary prime triples grow from `38` at `101` to `3105` at `1999`; polygonal atoms with sides `3..8` have max depths exactly `3..8` through `600`; Zeckendorf examples record unique no-adjacent supports such as `1000=F15+F6`.
**Predictions:** Representation systems should be measured by entropy `log |R_h(N)|`, local obstruction rank, and carry/conflict graph width. The shared axis is `redundancy -> bounded basis -> canonical peel`: Hardy-Littlewood abundance, Fermat bounded residue absorption, Zeckendorf entropy-zero confluence.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1962-additive-basis-entropy-ladder.md`, `04-computation/goldbach_polygonal_zeckendorf_bridge_s501.py`, `05-knowledge/results/goldbach_polygonal_zeckendorf_bridge_s501.out`, `07-reflections/goldbach-polygonal-zeckendorf-s501.md`, `07-reflections/summand-graph-fermat-zeckendorf.md`, `07-reflections/natural-operation-graphs-s365.md`.

## HYP-1963: Additive basis theorems split into smoothing, bounded-arity, and normal-form regimes (codex-2026-06-01-S501b)
**Status:** OPEN; structural synthesis refining HYP-1962's entropy ladder into proof currencies.
**What:** Additive-basis problems should be classified by proof currency. Goldbach/Hardy-Littlewood use dense prime atoms and many representations; Helfgott's ternary theorem buys an extra smoothing variable. Fermat polygonal numbers use polynomially sparse atoms but bounded arity `k`. Zeckendorf uses exponentially sparse atoms but a unique no-adjacent carry normal form.
**Evidence:** `additive_basis_goldbach_zeckendorf_s501.py` checks `N=5000`: no missing Goldbach parity targets, Hardy-Littlewood-shaped binary counts, stable ternary normalization by `n^2/log(n)^3`, polygonal max-term counts exactly `3..8` for `3`- through `8`-gonal bases, and Zeckendorf supports with complete no-adjacent normal forms. Residue tests show polygonal bounded sums and Zeckendorf automata cover all residues mod `2..18`, while prime unit sums keep parity-local restrictions.
**Predictions:** LRC and Tournament Analysis proof attempts should declare whether they are smoothing, bounded-row-certificate, or normal-form/carry arguments. The n=14/n=18 bridge-fiber work likely needs bounded-arity row weights followed by Zeckendorf/Ostrowski-style debt normal form.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1963-additive-basis-smoothing-normal-form-trichotomy.md`, HYP-1962, HYP-1822, HYP-1902, HYP-1920, `04-computation/additive_basis_goldbach_zeckendorf_s501.py`, `05-knowledge/results/additive_basis_goldbach_zeckendorf_s501.out`, `07-reflections/goldbach-polygonal-zeckendorf-additive-bases-s501.md`, `07-reflections/summand-graph-fermat-zeckendorf.md`, `07-reflections/fugacity-axis-and-vanishing-theorem.md`.

## HYP-1964: holdback/twin-prime/staircase axis of the tournament clock (oracle-2026-06-01-S25)
**Status:** EXPLORATORY with proved/computed parts. Tournament Analysis (continues S24/HYP-1951).
**What:** Holdback of a tournament-clock edge = 1/(2 d_ij) (persistence; d_ij=speed difference); spectrum = reciprocal difference-multiset; max holdback=1/(2 min_diff). CLAIMS: (a) the LRC-extremal set {1..n} has difference multiplicities n-1,...,1 = the staircase delta, so LRC-extremal=max-holdback=minimal-clock(S24)=staircase; (b) twin primes (d=2) are the stickiest edges odd primes allow (min_diff floor 2), the prime analogue of consecutive integers, sole diff-1 exception (2,3); (c) RESIDUE-MISSING DUALITY: by THM-369 a set is LRC-easy via t=1/m iff it misses residue 0 mod m; consecutive ints cover all residues (anti-admissible, LRC-tight), primes miss them (admissible-like, LRC-easy) -- so LRC-hardness=divisibility-rich, prime constellations=admissible/divisibility-poor, the SAME residue-missing feature serving twin primes and runner-ease; (d) equal differences flip in lockstep => #distinct differences=clock complexity (AP minimal, Sidon maximal).
**Evidence:** `tournament_clock_holdback_twinprime_s25.py` (+ .out): consecutive 1..6 holdbacks 1/2:5,1/4:4,1/6:3,1/8:2,1/10:1; odd primes 3..19 min_diff=2 max-holdback 1/4 LRC-easy(gap 3/76); consecutive 1..7 covers [2..7] LRC-tight(gap 0); primes miss 4,6 LRC-easy.
**Predictions:** holdback-spectrum({1..n})=staircase delta_{n-1} exactly; clock cell-count ~ Sigma d = staircase area; only scalar multiples of {1..n} are simultaneously high-holdback and anti-admissible; the alpha=1/k comparator turns "covers residue 0 mod k" into a clock event.
**See:** `07-reflections/holdback-twin-primes-and-the-staircase-s25.md`, `04-computation/tournament_clock_holdback_twinprime_s25.py`, HYP-1951, THM-369, `07-reflections/everything-is-the-triangle.md`.

## HYP-1966: twin primes are the base ray of the prime-pair plane (codex-2026-06-01-S495)
**Status:** OPEN; pair-first synthesis plus exact small probe.
**What:** Write every unordered pair as `(m-h,m+h)`. Twin primes are the fixed ray `h=1`; Goldbach fixes the midpoint row `m=N/2`; Hardy-Littlewood prime-pair factors are local residue products of the same slice; Zeckendorf carry is a normal-form debt on the pair edge.
**Evidence:** `pair_lens_twin_primes_s495.py` counts twin pairs and fixed-gap prime pairs through `200000`, reproducing the qualitative Hardy-Littlewood chord pattern (`h=3` ratio `1.988` vs predicted `2`, `h=15` ratio `2.679` vs predicted `8/3`). The midpoint sieve records forbidden residues `m=+/-h mod q`, explaining the twin-prime `6Z` midpoint spine and the gap-`2h` boost when `q|h`. Goldbach appears as the orthogonal fixed-midpoint slice, while the twin Zeckendorf scan finds no zero-carry twin pairs up to `50000`.
**Predictions:** Additive and LRC searches should first emit pair movies in `(midpoint, half-gap, local product, carry debt)`. Build pair tournaments over candidate gaps or representations by orienting the edge with lower residual debt. Bounded prime-gap theorems should be read as survival of a bounded ray family; twin primes ask for survival of the smallest ray `h=1`.

## HYP-1970: H is a half-turn loneliness meter, not a scalar max-gap meter (oracle-2026-06-01-S26; codex-2026-06-01-S26b)
**Status:** EXPLORATORY with computed/near-proved parts. Tournament Analysis (continues HYP-1951/1967).
**What:** For a half-turn circular tournament, `H=1` exactly detects the open-semicircle state in the nondegenerate case (`max circular gap > 1/2`) and the repo's tie-completed boundary condition `max circular gap >= 1/2`. Above this endpoint, H is a circular-tournament cell invariant, not a scalar loneliness score: it is not determined by max gap and is not pointwise monotone in max gap. It remains useful because it is correlated with spread and sees cyclic arrangement data lost by score and 3-cycle summaries.
**Evidence:** `H_loneliness_meter_s26.py` samples `n=5..9`: zero `H=1`/tie-completed `1/2-gap` mismatches; rounded max-gap buckets with multiple H values occur in `26/74`, `30/73`, `33/70`, `34/63`, and `36/57` buckets for `n=5..9`; pointwise monotonicity witnesses include `n=7` with `H=105,max_gap=0.1812` versus higher `H=151,max_gap=0.4881`. H separates score/c3 collisions (`n=6` H=41/45 and `n=7` H=123/137), while two-neighbor safe counts at `1/n` are extra data. In n=14 hard LRC rows, H remains high (`0.92`, `0.74`, `0.74` of initial) even though the anchored endpoint clock is the proof object.
**Predictions:** #3cyc=non-semicircle-triple count is provable from the three-arc characterization; the H-finer-than-score feature is a reach-word/arc-arrangement invariant; an `alpha=1/k` comparator family should recover exact threshold readings below `1/2`, but LRC will still need anchored endpoint data.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1970-h-half-turn-loneliness-meter.md`, `07-reflections/H-as-a-loneliness-meter-s26.md`, `04-computation/H_loneliness_meter_s26.py`, `05-knowledge/results/H_loneliness_meter_s26.out`, HYP-1951, HYP-1967, THM-374.

**Status:** OPEN; exact clock-cell and selected LRC-row audit.
**What:** `H(T)` has two loneliness readings in runner-clock tournaments. Low `H`, especially `H=1`, detects unanchored open-semicircle bunching by THM-374. High `H` often detects LRC-style even separation, where many vertices have two adjacent circular gaps at least `1/n`. The LRC invariant is therefore not scalar `H` alone; it is a marked tournament movie with circular order, stationary vertex, safe-gap mask, locally lonely vertices, and pressure/deletion data.
**Evidence:** `h_loneliness_meter_s506.py` finds strong negative `H` versus max-gap correlations in sampled clocks (`-0.946` for initial `n=5`, `-0.902` for prime `n=5`, `-0.912` for initial `n=6`, `-0.716` for spread `n=7`) while initial LRC witnesses `t=1/n` for total `n=5..9` have all vertices lonely and high `H` (`15,41,175,629,3267`). At selected `n=14` and `n=18` rows, `t=1/n` is high-H and all-lonely, whereas `t=1/(2n)` has `H=1`, a huge gap, and no marked stationary loneliness.
**Predictions:** Future LRC scripts should report `H` with maximum gap, marked stationary distance, safe-gap mask, locally lonely vertices, and pressure DAG/SCC status. Counterexample-shaped searches should prioritize marked pressure structure and local safe-gap masks over scalar `H`.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1971-h-loneliness-two-scale-meter.md`, HYP-1951, HYP-1941, HYP-1950, HYP-1960, THM-370, THM-374, `04-computation/h_loneliness_meter_s506.py`, `05-knowledge/results/h_loneliness_meter_s506.out`, `07-reflections/h-as-loneliness-meter-s506.md`.

## HYP-1972: LRC loneliness needs an arc-criteria metric vector (codex-2026-06-01-S506b)
**Status:** OPEN; exact small-clock scorecard plus selected n=14/n=18 row audit.
**What:** The useful LRC Tournament Analysis object is not one arc rule but a metric vector: `phase_H`, `phase_score_width`, marked origin scores for local-moat/deficit gauges, close-sector strict/tie rate, origin-blocker strict/tie shape, pressure largest SCC, pressure triangles, and edge-flip rate.
**Evidence:** `lrc_arc_criteria_loneliness_s506.py` tests twelve arc criteria. Best small-clock pairings are close-sector `tie_rate -> safe_gap_count` (mean |rho| `0.881`), local-moat marked origin score -> origin clearance (`0.821`), phase-half `H -> max_gap` (`0.790`), safe-deficit marked origin score -> origin clearance (`0.758`), and local-moat-sum marked origin score -> origin clearance (`0.689`). Selected hard rows keep pressure SCC size `1` and strict pressure triangles `0` in the sampled gauges.
**Predictions:** Counterexample-shaped LRC rows should show both endpoint debt and a nontrivial pressure event, not just small scalar gap. Future LRC scripts should emit the full metric vector across selected times and clock corridors.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1972-lrc-arc-criteria-loneliness-metric-vector.md`, `04-computation/lrc_arc_criteria_loneliness_s506.py`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_arc_criteria_loneliness_s506.out`, `07-reflections/lrc-arc-criteria-loneliness-s506.md`, HYP-1971, HYP-1967.

## HYP-1974: LRC loneliness metrics need endpoint-aware tournament gauges (codex-2026-06-01-S507)
**Status:** OPEN; gauge atlas plus finite benchmark.
**What:** For LRC, `H` becomes a useful loneliness metric only together with the gauge that produced the arcs. Pure half-turn is a sharp `1/2`-gap meter, but the LRC threshold needs endpoint-aware gauges: status-dominance gauges for witness detection, theta-close/antipodal-open switches for shape, and endpoint-wall/slack-band gauges for boundary pressure.
**Evidence:** `lrc_loneliness_tournament_gauges_s507.py` tests ten gauges over six small speed families. Unsafe/safe dominance gives H+score pure fraction `0.996`, safe isolation `0.845`, and unsafe isolation `0.866`. Theta-close has mixed-7 safe isolation `0.767`; antipodal-open has mixed-7 safe isolation `0.433` and the smallest H+score span among the top shape gauges. Pure half-turn safe isolation is only `0.028`.
**Predictions:** Future LRC scripts should report endpoint-aware tournament fingerprints beside scalar gaps: status-dominance score sequence, theta-close H/score width, antipodal-open SCC count, endpoint-wall pressure layers, and edge flips across lonely corridors. A genuine hard corridor should show stable shape-gauge signatures, not just isolated safe times.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1974-lrc-endpoint-aware-tournament-gauges.md`, `04-computation/lrc_loneliness_tournament_gauges_s507.py`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_loneliness_tournament_gauges_s507.out`, `07-reflections/lrc-loneliness-tournament-gauges-s507.md`, HYP-1931, HYP-1951, HYP-1967, HYP-1968.

## HYP-1973: LRC Tournament Analysis needs ranker, entropy, and pressure gauges (codex-2026-06-01-S506)
**Status:** OPEN; hard-row gauge lab.
**What:** No single LRC arc-assignment rule should carry the whole proof. Useful gauges split into ranker gauges (scalar coordinates, H collapses), phase/entropy gauges (global cyclic shape, H meaningful), and pressure gauges (blocker-debt hierarchy where score/SCC/source-sink shape matters more than completed H).
**Evidence:** `lrc_tournament_gauge_lab_s506.py` audits twelve gauges. The half-turn phase gauge sees the recursive plateau (`n14 H_ratio 2.0831 -> 1.6752 -> 1.6752`; `n18 2.3981 -> 2.0965 -> 2.0965`). Scalar rankers have one H value, no cyclic triples, and SCC size `1`. `safe_phase_gate` keeps spread correlation but can collapse H aggressively; `pressure_k2` has cyclic triples `0..40` and largest SCC `1..18`.
**Predictions:** Future LRC scans should report `(H_ratio(phase_half), H_ratio(safe_phase_gate), score_width(pressure_k2), largest_SCC(pressure_k2), endpoint_debt, gap*endpoint_debt)`. A counterexample-shaped row should break phase, endpoint-safe, and pressure layers at once.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1973-lrc-gauge-trichotomy.md`, `04-computation/lrc_tournament_gauge_lab_s506.py`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_tournament_gauge_lab_s506.out`, `07-reflections/lrc-tournament-gauge-lab-s506.md`, HYP-1967, HYP-1968, HYP-1969, THM-373, THM-380.

## HYP-1975: LRC loneliness is a gauge bundle, not a single tournament (codex-2026-06-01-S506c)
**Status:** OPEN; computed arc-gauge zoo on n=14/n=18 selected rows.
**What:** LRC Tournament Analysis should keep a bundle of arc-assignment gauges instead of promoting one tournament to the whole loneliness metric. The useful channels are global spread (`phase_H`, open-arc density), local `1/n` crowding (close-pair, danger-band, kinetic-close switches), pressure peeling (blocker/two-neighbor/threshold-deficit relief SCCs), marked observer data (observer outdegree/two-guard), and labelled endpoint debt. Scalar ranks are readable diagnostics but usually transitive; runner-level endpoint summaries are too coarse and should stay labelled.
**Evidence:** `lrc_arc_gauge_zoo_s506.py` evaluates n=14/n=18 initial, row-parent, gate, and double-gate rows. On n=14, phase H stays high but informative (`24104937 -> 22168229 -> 17826951`), with time-sample correlations `Hratio +0.393`, `c3 +0.416` against observer margin. Open-arc density distinguishes row-parent/gate distortion (`H=2952757` then `452385`, c3 correlation `+0.403`). Local crowding switches and relief gauges retain different nontransitive structures: `danger_band` has row/gate `c3=71/71`; `two_neighbor_relief` has `H=183523` then `55701`; `blocker_relief` drops to low-H pressure shapes (`3601` then `161`). Scalar ranks, velocity-only escape, and speed-level endpoint summaries collapse to transitive tournaments on selected rows.
**Predictions:** Counterexample-shaped rows should trigger a bundle event, not just lower scalar gap: e.g. pressure `largest_scc > 1` after endpoint-private peeling, a labelled endpoint cycle invisible in speed-level ranks, or a sharp conflict between open-arc density and local danger-band cycles. Future endpoint gauges should operate on endpoint-runner pair-cells before projection.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1975-lrc-arc-gauge-bundle.md`, `04-computation/lrc_arc_gauge_zoo_s506.py`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_arc_gauge_zoo_s506.out`, `07-reflections/lrc-arc-gauge-zoo-s506.md`, HYP-1932, HYP-1940, HYP-1967, HYP-1968, HYP-1970, HYP-1971, HYP-1972, HYP-1973, HYP-1974.

## HYP-1976: LRC needs pair-cell operation-grid Tournament Analysis (codex-2026-06-01-S509)
**Status:** OPEN; second-order pair-cell scorecard plus A000568/operation-grid synthesis.
**What:** The LRC metric vector should have two layers: runner-level arc criteria plus pair-cell operation-grid criteria. Pair-cell tournaments use unordered runner pairs as vertices and compare dynamic danger deficits, phase distances, wall frequencies, dyadic rows, odd cores, same odd doubling chains, and product-sum critical-pair defects.
**Evidence:** `lrc_pair_cell_operation_grid_s509.py` finds the strongest new pair-cell metric is `edge_danger_deficit`, whose tie rate tracks close-pair count with mean |rho| `0.986`; `edge_phase_distance` gives a weaker `origin_pair_mean_norm -> max_gap` signal (`0.536`). Static arithmetic criteria are not time-loneliness meters but stable branch labels. The script also verifies A000568 from the odd-partition Burnside formula and connects that odd-core survival to the LRC `x+2`/`x*2` grid and product-sum equation `(a-1)(b-1)=k-1`.
**Predictions:** Corridor-level LRC scripts should emit the S506b runner vector and the S509 pair-cell vector together. Static dyadic/odd/product-sum pair-cell criteria should separate families across denominators better than times within one family.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1976-lrc-pair-cell-operation-grid.md`, `04-computation/lrc_pair_cell_operation_grid_s509.py`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_pair_cell_operation_grid_s509.out`, `07-reflections/lrc-pair-cell-operation-grid-s509.md`, HYP-1972, HYP-1963, HYP-1965, HYP-1966.

## HYP-1977: LRC is a projection-defect problem over the A000568 quotient (codex-2026-06-01-S509b)
**Status:** OPEN; exact small-cell projection audit plus route Tournament Analysis.
**What:** LRC is analogous to A000568 through a quotient tower, not through a scalar property of plain tournament isomorphism classes. A speed-set movie maps to labelled half-turn/threshold tournaments, then to observer-pointed classes, then to unmarked A000568 classes. LRC safety is a section condition in the endpoint/gap fiber over that base.
**Evidence:** `lrc_a000568_iso_analogy_s509.py` samples exact time cells for small primitive speed sets and canonicalizes the half-turn phase tournament. Safe/unsafe cells mix inside unmarked classes for `N=4,5,6,7` with mixed counts `1,3,1,5`; they also mix inside pointed classes with counts `2,6,4,5`. Examples include `(2,3,5,7)` at `N=5`, where safe `t=3/28` and unsafe `t=67/560` share the same regular unmarked class with `H=15` and score `(2,2,2,2,2)`.
**Predictions:** Future LRC proof searches should treat `G_N` as the base path and endpoint/gap labels as sheaf data over it. Counterexample-shaped rows are quotient walks whose visited fibers are all endpoint-forbidden. Mixed fibers are repair opportunities. Initial consecutive speed sets should be handled as boundary sections of the staircase geodesic, not as safe open cells.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1977-lrc-a000568-projection-defect.md`, `04-computation/lrc_a000568_iso_analogy_s509.py`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_a000568_iso_analogy_s509.out`, `07-reflections/lrc-a000568-isoclass-analogy-s509.md`, HYP-1976, HYP-1975, HYP-1967, HYP-1970, THM-372, THM-373, THM-374.

## HYP-1978: LRC is a marked A000568-style quotient problem (codex-2026-06-01-S507)
**Status:** OPEN; quotient-atlas computation plus proof-program synthesis.
**What:** The A000568 analogy should be rooted/marked: ordinary tournament isomorphism classes form the unrooted base, while LRC counterexamples would be bad orbits in a rooted, endpoint-marked species containing the stationary observer, safe masks, endpoint-owner/protection labels, pressure labels, and tie Hamiltonian path.
**Evidence:** `lrc_a000568_isoclass_atlas_s507.py` computes exact small quotients. Rooted classes already expand the base (`n=6`: A000568 `56`, rooted `296`), while H and score are coarse (`n=6`: `19` H buckets, `22` score buckets). The initial LRC clock is a thin quotient walk (`n=7`: `24` cells visit `7` unrooted and `17` rooted states out of A000568 `456`). On hard ladders, `phase_half` gives the same coarse rooted shadow for `n14-s14/s28` and `n18-s18/s36` while endpoint debt doubles.
**Predictions:** There should be a useful rooted/colored Burnside or fixed-point-killing theorem for the marked LRC species. A true bad class must combine no positive gap, no unprotected endpoint, safe-phase alarm, pressure_k2 SCC carrying the endpoint core, and non-harmless phase H.

## HYP-1979: LRC clock images are sparse marked walks inside the A000568 chamber space (codex-2026-06-01-S510)
**Status:** OPEN; exact small-clock isomorphism audit plus n=14/n=18 fingerprint shadow.
**What:** In the HYP-1977 projection-defect frame, the half-turn runner clock occupies a tiny sparse arithmetic image inside the A000568 chamber space. The unmarked chamber is the base, the observer-fixed canonical class is a marked lift, and exact safe witness samples are target hits that must still retain endpoint/gap labels before being used in an LRC proof.
**Evidence:** `lrc_a000568_marked_chamber_s510.py` computes A000568 by the odd-cycle Burnside formula, canonicalizes small runner clocks exactly, and shows sparsity: `N5 initial` visits `4/12` unmarked classes, `N6 initial` visits `4/56`, `N7 initial` visits `7/456`, and `N7 prime-like` visits `10/456` with `55` observer-marked classes. Large-row shadows remain microscopic relative to A000568: n14 row-parent has `1772` cells and `140` coarse fingerprints versus A000568(14)=`28401423719122304`; n18 row-parent has `4008` cells and `304` fingerprints versus A000568(18)=`1783398846284777975419600287232`.

## HYP-1980: LRC operation-grid labels become metrics when weighted by current danger (codex-2026-06-01-S511)
**Status:** OPEN; operation-weighted runner arc criteria over exact small clocks and selected hard rows.
**What:** Static `x+2`/`x*2` operation-grid labels are branch coordinates, not time-loneliness meters. They become useful LRC fiber metrics when weighted by current pair-cell danger `max(0,1/N-dist(i,j,t))`, producing dyadic, odd-chain, additive, multiplicative, and product-sum danger gauges over the marked A000568 quotient base.
**Evidence:** `lrc_operation_grid_arc_criteria_s511.py` tests twenty arc criteria. The exact small-clock leaders are `s506_lrc_close_sector` (`tie_rate -> close_pair_count`, mean |rho| `1.000`), `additive_danger_interface` (`tie_rate -> safe_gap_count`, `0.882`), `product_sum_danger` (`0.840`), `local_two_guard` (`0.837`), `incident_danger_sum` (`0.815`), and `s506_phase_half` (`H -> max_gap`, `0.798`). Pure static operation labels have zero within-clock Spearman signal, confirming they are fixed coordinates. A criteria-choice tournament ranks the hybrid operation-danger gauges as the top five profile winners.
**Predictions:** Corridor scripts should carry `phase_H`, close-sector tie rate, incident danger origin score, dyadic/additive/multiplicative/product-sum danger score histograms, strict pressure SCCs, and endpoint-labelled brackets. Mixed A000568 fibers should often be explained by operation-weighted repair channels; a counterexample-shaped row must keep all such fibers endpoint-forbidden.

## HYP-1981: LRC = source-reachability in the observer-marked A000568 quotient (oracle-2026-06-01-S511)
**Status:** PARTLY CANONIZED; THM-381 proves the observer-source equivalence and `A000568(n-1)` target count. Reachable-source classification remains open.
**What:** Mark the observer (speed 0); orient observer->runner i iff ||v_i t||>=1/n (runner in safe arc [1/n,1-1/n]), runner-runner by half-turn. Then EXACTLY observer-lonely <=> observer is a SOURCE (out-deg n-1). So LRC(observer) <=> the observer-marked tournament clock (LRC walls) reaches a cell whose marked class has the observer as a source -- a pure marked tournament-iso-class (A000568-with-mark) reachability statement. The source-marked target = tournaments on the n-1 runners = A000568(n-1) classes EXACTLY (n=4->2,...,8->456). Counterexample <=> the marked walk avoids all source classes; tight <=> source only on the boundary (initial segments + sporadic {1,3,4,5,9} have 0 open source cells); LRC-easy <=> >=1 open source cell. Reachable source classes = circular (n-1)-tournaments in an arc of length 1-2/n (tiny subset of A000568(n-1)). Addition drives the walk (LRC endpoint walls, staircase of differences); multiplication shapes space+target (A000568 odd-cycle Burnside; Paley/QR speed-difference tournaments).
**Evidence:** `lrc_observer_source_tournament_s511.py` (mism=0 n=4,5,6; initial/sporadic-tight have 0 open source cells; (0,1,2,5),(0,2,3,5,7) have 4,6); `lrc_qr_paley_speed_tournament_s511.py` (Paley p=3,7,11 regular H=3,189,95095; A000568(n-1) target verified).
**Predictions:** the reachable source-class count (circular (n-1)-tournaments in arc 1-2/n) is the true LRC target, far smaller than A000568(n-1); the extremal set is the unique boundary-only source-toucher; Paley/Burnside menu ties to the sieve THM-369 residue conditions.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1981-lrc-observer-source-reachability.md`, `01-canon/theorems/THM-381-lrc-observer-source-marked-reachability.md`, `07-reflections/lrc-as-source-reachability-in-marked-A000568-s511.md`, HYP-1977, HYP-1979, HYP-1951, THM-369, `07-reflections/lrc-a000568-isoclass-analogy-s509.md`.

## HYP-1982: LRC is a forced visit in a threshold-decorated tournament-class fiber (codex-2026-06-01-S512)
**Status:** OPEN; exact bounded small-n fiber audit canonized as THM-382/THM-383.
**What:** The LRC/A000568 analogy should be formulated over decorated fibers, not raw unmarked tournament classes: ordinary iso class plus circular/tie-wall stratum, observer mark, and `1/n` threshold colors on gaps or pair-cells. A lonely time is a visit to a good-only fiber.
**Evidence:** `lrc_iso_class_constraint_s512.py` verifies A000568 values by Burnside/direct enumeration, then tests total `n=3,4`. Raw phase, observer-marked phase, gap-rank-only, and pair-deficit-rank-only classes are mixed and certify no sampled speed set. Threshold-decorated fibers separate: for total `n=3`, `gap_threshold_fiber` has `8` classes with `2` good-only and certifies `79/79`; `pair_deficit_threshold_fiber` has `9` classes with `2` good-only and certifies `79/79`. For total `n=4`, gap threshold has `20` classes with `5` good-only and certifies `109/109`; pair threshold has `50` classes with `4` good-only and certifies `109/109`.
**Predictions:** Raw A000568 class remains too coarse at larger `n`; an `n=14` proof by this route should avoid full A000568(14) enumeration and instead prove that the hard quotient-ladder walk in a compressed gap/pair/endpoint-pressure fiber must hit a good-only threshold class.

## HYP-1983: LRC operation arc rules split into ledgers, phase perturbations, and marked bundle coordinates (codex-2026-06-01-S511c)

## HYP-1984: LRC loneliness is an add/multiply gauge stack over the odd-core grid (codex-2026-06-01-S513)
**Status:** OPEN; denominator Tournament Analysis plus synthesis.

## HYP-1985: The LRC stack obstruction needs an owner-compatible pressure core (codex-2026-06-01-S514)
**Status:** OPEN; three-layer row/state stack audit.
**What:** The S513 add/multiply stack ranks LRC hard rows, but a counterexample-shaped obstruction cannot be certified by coarse row-time pressure SCCs alone. The pressure coordinate must keep the labelled owner-compatible endpoint core from THM-380; arithmetic scarcity and operation danger without that core are hard-row texture, not obstruction.
**Evidence:** `lrc_three_layer_stack_audit_s514.py` audits 11 selected n14/n16/n18 rows. The full-stack row tournament is nontrivial (`H=87`, `c3=10`, `SCCs=(8,1,1,1)`), led by n14 double-gate, n16 gate, n18 double-gate, and n18 gate rows. Runner dynamics and denominator labels flip `0.45` of row edges; operation-weighted danger and denominator labels flip `0.53`; full stack sits close to operation-weighted danger (`0.07` flips). The 38-state tournament has `c3=2`, `SCCs=(4,1,1,...)`.
**Refinement:** The conjunction audit gives n14/n16 rows `4/5` flags and n18 rows `3/5` flags for additive scarcity, endpoint debt, product-sum collisions, A000568 survival, and pressure. The missing flag is always pressure: no sampled row has a nontrivial coarse pressure SCC.
**Predictions:** Future stack scripts should replace the coarse pressure coordinate with THM-380 owner-compatible endpoint-core data. A true counterexample-shaped row must align runner danger, operation-weighted pair-cell danger, denominator add/multiply/A000568 labels, and a nonpeeling labelled endpoint-pressure core.

## HYP-1994: twin-prime Goldbach = binary Goldbach on the twin-center necklace; last exception 4208 (oracle-2026-06-01-S521)
**Status:** OPEN (structural reduction VERIFIED exactly; finiteness-tail conjecture open). Detail: `HYP-1994-twin-goldbach-necklace.md`.
**What:** Even numbers not a sum of two twin primes = OEIS A007534 = exactly **35** (largest **4208**), computed to 2e6. Apart from {2,4} they are **11 triples** {6m-2,6m,6m+2}. WHY: twin pairs center on multiples of 6 (C⊂6Z, only {3,5} anomalous) so a sum of two twin primes ∈ {c1+c2-2,c1+c2,c1+c2+2}, c1+c2∈6Z — each mod-6 triple is representable as a UNIT iff its center 6m∈C+C. VERIFIED (0 mismatches, even n∈(8,6000]): n twin-Goldbach ⇔ round(n/6)∈K+K, K=C/6={k:6k±1 twin}={1,2,3,5,7,10,12,...}. The 11 triples ⇔ the 11 holes of K+K: m=16,67,86,131,151,186,191,211,226,541,**701** (10 of 11 ≡1 mod 5, noted by concurrent S516). CONJECTURE: 701 is the last hole (no exception >4208); K sparser than primes ⇒ holes are a finite startup deficit. Lens link: 6k = HYP-1965/1966 pair-lens coordinate. Evidence: `04-computation/twin_goldbach_exceptions_s521.py`, `twin_goldbach_necklace_reduction_s521.py`. Reflections: `07-reflections/twin-goldbach-necklace-triples-s521.md`, `twin-goldbach-35-exceptions-the-six-wheel-s516.md`.

## HYP-1987: the true LRC target is the arc-confined circular menu in A000568(n-1) (oracle-2026-06-01-S512)
**Status:** EXPLORATORY; re-proves LRC for n<=7 computationally. Sharpens HYP-1981.
**What:** In the S511 observer-marked reformulation (LRC <=> marked walk reaches a SOURCE class, target A000568(n-1)), the REACHABLE source classes are exactly half-turn tournaments of n-1 points in the fixed safe arc [1/n,1-1/n] of length L=1-2/n -- a tiny subset of A000568(n-1). Computed: reachable source classes 1,2,6,6 for n=4,5,6,7 vs A000568(n-1)=2,4,12,56; 0 LRC failures over all primitive speed sets in a box (n=4:325, n=5:479, n=6:461, n=7:210) => the tournament framing RE-PROVES LRC for n<=7. For L<=1/2 (n<=4) the reachable source class is forced transitive; for n>=5 non-transitive (H>1) source classes appear, menu grows with L. EQUIVALENT PROBLEM (LRC-T): every primitive speed set's observer-marked clock visits a cell whose runner sub-tournament lies in this arc-confined menu. The missing piece = the additive walk cannot avoid this tiny multiplicatively-shaped (A000568 odd-Burnside / Paley / sieve THM-369) target.
**Evidence:** `lrc_source_reachability_deep_s512.py` (+ .out): counts above, 0 failures, forced-transitive for n<=4, min-near-runner histograms.
**Predictions:** reachable-menu sequence 1,2,6,6,... = "half-turn tournaments of m points in arc 1-2/(m+1)"; few-distinct-difference families provable via staircase synchrony (S25); the target is a vanishing fraction of A000568(n-1) so an avoidance/counterexample walk threads an enormous complement (the tournament shadow of the measure/overlap tension).
**See:** `07-reflections/lrc-tournament-analysis-capstone-s512.md`, HYP-1981, HYP-1951, HYP-1964, HYP-1968, THM-369, HYP-1977.
**UPDATE (oracle-2026-06-01-S520):** extended the menu to n=8 and box-saturation-tested all terms. (a) LRC re-verified on the tournament side for **n=8** (0 source-avoidance failures over boxes of 36/330/792 primitive sets). (b) Menu sizes for n<=7 are **box-stable** (n=5:2 to ms=26; n=6:6 to ms=15; n=7:6 to ms=14) — strong evidence they are the true menu sizes, NOT single-box artifacts. (c) **n=8 is only a LOWER BOUND >=12**: the snapshot count 11 grew to 12 (new class H=24) when the box went 11->12, so a single-box menu count is a lower bound until saturation is shown — restate the "exact finite menu" as "box-stable menu". (d) STRUCTURAL: reachable menu = **A000568(n-1)/2 exactly** for n=4,5,6 (1,2,6) then **collapses at n=7** (6 vs 28) and n=8 (>=12 vs 228) — the vanishing fraction switches on sharply at n=7 (same threshold as the width-formula failure / E_7 odd holes), not gradually. Evidence: `04-computation/lrc_source_reachability_n8_s520.py`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_source_reachability_n8_s520.out`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_source_menu_saturation_s520.out`. Reflection: `07-reflections/lrc-source-menu-collapses-at-n7-s520.md`.
**UPDATE (codex-2026-06-01-S525):** separated the open source menu from the compactified wall menu. In open source cells, ordering the `m=n-1` runners in the safe arc makes backward edges a Ferrers filter; the fixed length `1-2/n` cuts Catalan-many filters down to interval-feasible menus. Open iso counts for `n=4..9` are `1,2,4,6,10,16`, agreeing with HYP-1996's circular `2*Fib` menu from `n>=5`, and bounded speed clocks match the geometric open menu exactly for `n=4..8`. The larger S512/S520 closed counts are boundary/tie-path additions. ## HYP-1988: Observer-score repair fibers around the source target (codex-2026-06-01-S517)
**Status:** OPEN; local observer-score stratification proved as THM-385, repair interpretation exploratory.
**What:** Around the HYP-1981 source target there is an exact observer-score stratification: `indeg(observer)` is the number of blocking runners. Thus source means zero blockers, almost-source means one blocker, and the `k`-blocker layer is `k` observer incident edge flips from source. Add side-defect labels and observer 2-king status to get a repair fiber: which forbidden cap carries debt, and whether every blocker is beaten by some currently safe runner.
**Evidence:** `lrc_observer_predicate_zoo_s517.py` checks exact open/wall samples in bounded `N=4,5,6,7` windows. It reports zero score mismatches across `21040`, `25816`, `18952`, and `4064` states. Marked classes never mix blocker counts; runner-subtournament classes do (`2`, `4`, `11`, `19` mixed runner-subclasses). The predicate tournament ranks source, almost-source, observer-score layer, side-defect layer, and endpoint-pressure core as the useful neighborhood of HYP-1981.
**Predictions:** Initial rows should remain wall-only source systems with open minimum blocker count `1`; almost-source and side-defect layers should classify tight states; observer 2-king should correlate with labelled pressure peelability; any counterexample-shaped walk must stay in positive blocker layers while avoiding all repair mechanisms that force a source wall or interval.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1988-lrc-observer-score-repair-fibers.md`, `01-canon/theorems/THM-385-lrc-observer-score-blocker-stratification.md`, `04-computation/lrc_observer_predicate_zoo_s517.py`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_observer_predicate_zoo_s517.out`, `07-reflections/lrc-observer-predicate-zoo-s517.md`, HYP-1987, HYP-1986, HYP-1981, THM-384, THM-381, THM-380.

## HYP-1989: n=145 CRT aperture proof route (codex-2026-06-01-S518)
**Status:** OPEN; n=145-specific proof strategy plus exact residue/trap audit.
**What:** For `n=145=5*29`, unit walls `t=a/145` make every nonzero residue modulo `145` safe; only speeds divisible by `145` block. If no speed is divisible by `145`, THM-369 solves. If a `145`-divisible blocker exists, the `144` moving-runner budget prevents occupancy of all nonzero residues, so some antipodal boundary pair `{r,-r}` has a missing side. Choosing `a^{-1}=r` creates a one-sided aperture. The proof target is to push the zero-residue embryo through an aperture or extract a THM-380 labelled pressure obstruction.
**Evidence:** `lrc_n145_whacky_reframes_s518.py` audits model families. `initial_1_to_144` has `q=145` blockers `0`, so it is solved by the unit wall. `unit_wall_with_one_zero` and `lcm_spike_units` have `q=145` blockers `1` and antipodal pairs `71/1/0`, exposing the one-sided aperture. The route tournament ranks `unit_wall_aperture`, `zero_residue_embryo`, `almost_source_tunnel`, `crt_5_29_two_moons`, and `observer_score_descent` as the leading proof languages.
**Predictions:** Any n=145 near-counterexample is sieve-complete and has a `145`-divisible blocker; residue occupancy itself cannot prevent a unit-wall aperture; the hard obstruction is dynamic aperture width versus the scaled zero-residue embryo; failed escape should leave labelled endpoint-pressure data.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1989-lrc-n145-crt-aperture-proof.md`, `04-computation/lrc_n145_whacky_reframes_s518.py`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_n145_whacky_reframes_s518.out`, `07-reflections/lrc-n145-whacky-reframes-s518.md`, HYP-1988, HYP-1986, HYP-1981, THM-385, THM-380, THM-369.
## HYP-1990: LRC gap-race entry forcing (opus-2026-06-01-S519)
**Status:** OPEN; THM-387 proves the structural flow, forced LL visit unproved.
**What:** THM-387 proves the LL fiber (lonely state) can only be entered via a "gap race" from LS: g_right must grow past 1/n before g_left drops below 1/n. The conjecture is that for any primitive speed set with n>=3, at least one of the O(sum v_i) wrap-around resets per period produces an LS entry where g_right wins. The gap-sum integral approach (∫(g_left+g_right) >= 2/n) is REFUTED at n>=5, so the proof must use the trajectory structure, not averaging.
**Evidence:** Exact bounded verification in `lrc_two_gap_dynamics_s518.py` confirms closed-LL hits throughout the stored `n=3,4,5,6` windows. The simplified no-swap race model scans 3385 primitive speed sets at `n=3,4,5,6` and predicts an LL race for all but wall/tie edge cases where compactification is needed. S522's swap-aware reset trace has zero full/race mismatches in the same windows and shows every successful first hit is a compactified `wall_LL`. For initial segments at n=14, 17/46 LS entries lead to LL in the simplified race. Gap exchange is strongly anti-correlated.
**Predictions:** The favorable wrap-around is one where the wrapping runner has enough left-gap runway relative to the active speeds and nearest-runner swaps. The arithmetic structure of primitive speed sets (sieve completeness, CRT) should guarantee such a reset, but the strong condition `L_0 > 1 - 1/n` is only sufficient, not necessary.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1990-lrc-gap-race-entry-forcing.md`, `01-canon/theorems/THM-387-lrc-two-gap-monotonicity-and-fiber-flow.md`, `04-computation/lrc_two_gap_dynamics_s518.py`, `04-computation/lrc_gap_race_analysis_s518.py`, `04-computation/lrc_gap_race_swap_s522.py`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_two_gap_dynamics_s518.out`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_gap_race_analysis_s518.out`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_gap_race_swap_s522.out`, `07-reflections/lrc-two-gap-monotonicity-dynamics-s519.md`, HYP-1995, HYP-1986, THM-387, THM-384.
## HYP-1991: n=14 has a bounded small-cofactor ansatz (oracle-2026-06-01-S514)
**Status:** EXPLORATORY; computationally supported, the adversarial regime is the open gap.
**What:** Every primitive 13-speed set (threshold 1/14) has a lonely time t=j/(14 s) for some cofactor s<=7, with s observed in {1,2,4,5,7} (small multiples of 14 echoing 14=2*7: {1,2,4}=2-part, {5,7}=small odd). If the cofactor is bounded by a small s_0 even adversarially, then with the minimal-counterexample speed bound this gives a finite-check proof of LRC@14 (the Rosenfeld / Sungkawichai-Trakulthongchai methodology). Companion ideas: anti-concentration (N(t)=#near, E[N]=13/7, generic P(N=0)~e^-13/7; only exact resonance reaches 0) and parity split (14=2*7 -> two proved LRC@<=7 halves coupled by t<->2t).
**Evidence:** `lrc_n14_wacky_attempts_s514.py` (+ .out): witness cofactor s<=5 across random+structured sets with speeds up to 1000; s does not grow with speed magnitude. Tight initial segment witness at s=1 (t=1/14, boundary).
**Predictions:** the cofactor stays bounded (small s_0) even for adversarial near-tight large-speed sets; the ansatz denominators 14*{1,2,4,5,7} tie to the sieve (THM-369) residue bands and the marked-source target (HYP-1987).

## HYP-1992: LRC rapidity threshold via the formal group F(x,y)=(x+y)/(1+xy) (opus-2026-06-01-S520)
**Status:** OPEN; quantitative bridge from H(T) to LRC via hyperbolic geometry.
**What:** Map each runner's circular distance g_i=||v_i t|| to rapidity r_i=atanh(1-2g_i). The formal group F(x,y)=(x+y)/(1+xy)=tanh(atanh(x)+atanh(y)) is addition in rapidity space. The LRC threshold g_i>=1/n becomes r_i<=0.5*ln(n-1), a Riemannian distance. The total rapidity budget is (n-1)*0.5*ln(n-1)=O(n*log(n)). This is the first quantitative connection between the project's central algebraic object (the formal group controlling H(T), fiber fractions, Walsh transforms) and the LRC lonely measure.
**Evidence:** `lrc_creative_reframes_s520.py` computes rapidity spectra at n=3..6 and the rapidity budget for n up to 100. The "relativistic energy" (product of cosh(r_i)) shows clean scaling. Initial-segment runners at t=1/(2n) do NOT all satisfy the rapidity bound (max rapidity exceeds threshold), confirming that 1/(2n) is a boundary witness, not an interior one.
**Predictions:** The hyperbolic-geometry language should unify the half-turn phase clock (THM-374), the observer-source condition (THM-381), and the gap-race framework (THM-387) via the formal group's rapidity metric. The Cayley-Dickson tower (n=2,3,5,9,17) may identify special LRC denominators where the rapidity argument simplifies.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1992-lrc-rapidity-formal-group-bridge.md`, `04-computation/lrc_creative_reframes_s520.py`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_creative_reframes_s520.out`, `07-reflections/lrc-creative-reframes-s520.md`, HYP-1990, THM-387, THM-384, THM-381.

## HYP-1997: the menu-metagraph walk is faithful but non-reducing; LRC = realizable-walk family (oracle-2026-06-01-S519)
**Status:** EXPLORATORY (honest negative). Clarifies the walk-on-metagraph program (HYP-1996).
**What:** LRC@n = a closed t-ordered runner-walk in the menu metagraph M_n (2*Fib(n-2) circular classes; edges=single wall-crossings) must hit a lonely class. The natural reduction "lonely classes are a feedback vertex set of M_n" FAILS: computed M_n is n=5 tree / n=6 6V7E(2 cycles) / n=7 10V13E(4 cycles), and transitive is NOT a FVS for n>=6. Two reasons it cannot reduce LRC: (a) closed walks backtrack so FVS != 'every closed walk hits lonely'; (b) realizable runner-walks are arithmetically special (edge (i,j) flips 2|v_i-v_j| times in a speed-determined order) and characterizing them IS LRC. So the metagraph is faithful+clarifying, not reducing. The walk meets the sieve (THM-369) exactly at the n-gon cells t=a/n; the residual is the fine q>n regime (S18) governed by the bounded-ansatz cofactor (S514, interval-exchange/wall-crossing order).
**Evidence:** lrc_menu_metagraph_fvs_s519.py (+ .out): M_n vertex/edge/cycle counts; FVS test False for n>=6.
**Predictions:** progress on n=14 needs the order-combinatorial (interval-exchange) structure of the realizable walk, not a pure graph property; M_n's cycle space (cyclomatic 0,2,4 for n=5,6,7) enumerates the non-bunching walk types.
**See:** 07-reflections/lrc-walk-on-metagraph-proof-attempt-s519.md, HYP-1996, HYP-1991(ansatz), THM-369, HYP-1987.

## HYP-1998: The LRC clock-movie realizes the round/A000016 necklace body (oracle-2026-06-01-S523)
**Status:** PARTIALLY-TRUE; open-time characterization verified for small `m`, boundary seam still open.
**What:** In the THM-381 observer-source movie, the runner sub-tournament at open times is round/locally-transitive/half-turn-realizable, so the generic realizable body is counted by A000016 rather than all of A000568. This gives the layer chain `A000568(n-1) ⊋ boundary-compactified ⊋ ROUND=A000016(n-1) ⊋ lonely menu`.
**Evidence:** `lrc_realizable_isoclasses_s523.py` verifies round equals locally-transitive equals half-turn-realizable for `m=3,4,5`; round iso counts for `m=3..7` are `2,2,4,6,10`, with the odd-divisor necklace formula predicting `16,30` for `m=8,9`. It explains why open clock images are tiny while boundary/tie-resolved classes can exceed the round body.
**Predictions:** Extend the round=A000016 verification to `m=8,9`; count the boundary-compactified realizable set; test whether the lonely menu is exactly the round body plus boundary source classes.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1998-lrc-realizable-round-necklace.md`, `04-computation/lrc_realizable_isoclasses_s523.py`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_realizable_isoclasses_s523.out`, `07-reflections/lrc-is-a-round-tournament-necklace-s523.md`, HYP-1987, HYP-1995, THM-381.

## HYP-1999: Fixed-n LRC source targets are Ferrers interval tournament menus (codex-2026-06-01-S525)
**Status:** OPEN; exact open-menu atlas and proof-language refinement of HYP-1987.
**What:** At fixed total denominator `n`, LRC is source reachability in an observer-marked tournament on `n` vertices; deleting the source observer leaves a runner tournament on `m=n-1` vertices. In an open source cell the runners lie in one arc of length `1-2/n`, so backward edges relative to arc order form a Ferrers filter. The fixed-`n` target is the interval-feasible Ferrers menu, plus compactified wall boundary classes, inside `A000568(n-1)`. This is the fixed-safe-arc refinement of HYP-1996's circular/Fibonacci menu and HYP-1998's round-necklace body, and it is the target-class counterpart to HYP-1997's metagraph-walk reduction.
**Evidence:** `lrc_fixed_n_tournament_class_atlas_s525.py` enumerates open menus `1,2,4,6,10,16` for `n=4..9` inside `A000568(n-1)=2,4,12,56,456,6880`. Feasible Ferrers signatures are `1,8,16,32,64,128`; bounded speed-clock scans for `n=4..8` match the geometric open menu exactly. The fixed-`n` profile Tournament Analysis is transitive (`c3=0`, singleton SCCs, one Hamiltonian path).
**Predictions:** The feasible-signature count should admit a direct `2^(n-2)` proof for `n>=5`; closed S512/S520 source classes should be exactly the THM-383 boundary compactification of the open Ferrers menu; hard frontiers such as `n=14` and `n=18` should be studied as forced visits to this tiny menu or its wall boundary, not to all raw A000568 classes.
**See:** `05-knowledge/hypotheses/HYP-1999-lrc-fixed-n-ferrers-interval-menu.md`, `04-computation/lrc_fixed_n_tournament_class_atlas_s525.py`, `05-knowledge/results/lrc_fixed_n_tournament_class_atlas_s525.out`, `07-reflections/lrc-fixed-n-ferrers-interval-menu-s525.md`, HYP-1981, HYP-1982, HYP-1987, HYP-1996, HYP-1997, HYP-1998, THM-381, THM-383.

## HYP-2000: arc-flips are dependent (recursive sub-ranking tree); LRC walk = 1-parameter sweep in tiling coordinates (oracle-2026-06-01-S520o)
**Status:** EXPLORATORY synthesis (computationally supported). Sharpens S519's obstruction.
**What:** The 2^C(n,2) independent-arc view misses the essence: a tournament is a ranking recursively built from sub-rankings (aligned=transitive/anti=cyclic), captured by the tiling model (base path + tiles = cut+cycle = score+cyclic; staircase recursion = sub-ranking tree). Evidence: (1) layers 2^C(n,2)->A000568->2*Fib(m-2) (free arcs vastly overcount the essence); (2) only 0.500/0.400/0.333 of single-arc-flips keep a circular tournament circular (n=5,6,7) -> realizable set is a thin dependent slice, not a subcube; (3) H=Tilings*|Aut|=#rankings sharing the arcs = the hidden coupling = the loneliness meter (S26). LRC: the runner walk's arc-flips are driven by ONE parameter t (all wall-crossings slaved to one clock), so realizable walks are a 1-parameter sweep -- why S519's pure-graph FVS reframe failed (flips aren't free). Right coordinates = tiling/recursive-tree basis; realizable walks = tile-toggle sequences from a 1-parameter circular sweep = interval-exchange in tiling basis = bounded-ansatz/wall-order (S514).
**Evidence:** arc_dependence_tiling_lrc_s520o.py (+ .out): dependence layers; arc-flip-stays-circular 0.5/0.4/0.333; circular menu H-values.
**Predictions:** characterizing realizable runner-walks needs the tiling/cut-cycle coordinates not free arcs; the fraction-staying-circular ->0 as n grows (realizability ever more fragile); the recursive sub-ranking tree is the natural basis for the LRC ansatz.
**See:** 07-reflections/arcs-are-not-independent-the-recursive-ranking-tree-s520o.md, HYP-1997, HYP-1996, HYP-1991, 'everything-is-the-triangle' reflection, THM-369.

Loneliness = a SECOND arrangement {x_i=±1/n}; LRC@n = the geodesic pierces the central box B=[1/n,1-1/n]^{n-1} (view-obstruction). Sharpening: inside each braid cell, order fixed => loneliness = a LINEAR interval condition; LRC = union of per-cell lonely sub-intervals nonempty = geometrized bounded-ansatz (S514).
